  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/.codex/skills/.system/gpt-image/SKILL.md\n+---\n+name: \"gpt-image\"\n+description: \"Generate images programmatically with OpenAI GPT Image models through the Image API. Use when the user explicitly asks for the OpenAI API/CLI path, prompt lists, repeat counts per prompt, dimensions, quality, format, compression, moderation, or saved image files from GPT Image.\"\n+---\n+\n+# GPT Image API Skill\n+\n+Use this skill when the task should call OpenAI's Image API directly instead of the built-in image tool.\n+\n+## Current API Notes\n+\n+Official docs checked on 2026-04-30:\n+\n+- Image generation endpoint: `POST /v1/images/generations`.\n+- Latest GPT Image model in the guide: `gpt-image-2`.\n+- Direct generation supports `prompt`, `model`, `n`, `size`, `quality`, `output_format`, `output_compression`, `background`, and `moderation`.\n+- `gpt-image-2` accepts custom `WIDTHxHEIGHT` sizes when both edges are multiples of 16, max edge is <= 3840px, aspect ratio is <= 3:1, and total pixels are 655,360 to 8,294,400.\n+- Popular sizes include `1024x1024`, `1536x1024`, `1024x1536`, `2048x2048`, `2048x1152`, `3840x2160`, `2160x3840`, and `auto`.\n+- `quality`: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `auto`.\n+- `output_format`: `png`, `jpeg`, or `webp`; `output_compression` applies to JPEG/WebP.\n+- `moderation`: `auto` or `low`.\n+- `gpt-image-2` does not support transparent backgrounds. Use `background=opaque` or `auto` for `gpt-image-2`; use an older supported model only when the user explicitly needs native transparent output.\n+\n+Primary sources:\n+\n+- https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/image-generation\n+- https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/images\n+\n+## Script\n+\n+Use:\n+\n+```bash\n+python \"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills/.system/gpt-image/scripts/gpt_image.py\" generate \\\n+  --prompt \"A clean editorial product photo of a ceramic mug\" \\\n+  --n 2 \\\n+  --size 1024x1024 \\\n+  --quality medium \\\n+  --output-format png \\\n+  --out-dir output/gpt-image\n+```\n+\n+For Claude, the same script is mirrored at:\n+\n+```bash\n+python \"$HOME/.claude/skills/gpt-image/scripts/gpt_image.py\" generate ...\n+```\n+\n+Batch prompt file formats:\n+\n+- Plain text: one prompt per non-empty line.\n+- JSON array: `[\"prompt one\", {\"prompt\": \"prompt two\", \"n\": 3, \"size\": \"1536x1024\"}]`\n+- JSONL: one string or object per line.\n+\n+Example:\n+\n+```bash\n+python \"${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills/.system/gpt-image/scripts/gpt_image.py\" generate \\\n+  --prompts-file prompts.jsonl \\\n+  --n 4 \\\n+  --size 2048x1152 \\\n+  --quality low \\\n+  --output-format jpeg \\\n+  --output-compression 80 \\\n+  --out-dir output/gpt-image \\\n+  --manifest output/gpt-image/manifest.json\n+```\n+\n+## API Key Handling\n+\n+Never hardcode or print the API key. The script resolves `OPENAI_API_KEY` in this order:\n+\n+1. Existing environment variable.\n+2. Any `--env-file PATH` passed explicitly.\n+3. Common local env files such as `.env` in the current directory or parents, `$HOME/.env`, `$HOME/.codex/.env`, `$HOME/.claude/.env`, and `$HOME/.hermes/.env`.\n+\n+If no key is found, ask the user to export `OPENAI_API_KEY` or pass `--env-file`.\n+\n+## Operating Rules\n+\n+- Use `--dry-run` before expensive batches unless the user explicitly says to run it.\n+- Keep `n` for variants of one prompt. Use prompt files for distinct assets.\n+- Prefer `quality=low` for drafts and `medium` or `high` for final assets.\n+- Save outputs in the current repo or a user-specified directory. json\n+import os\n+from pathlib import Path\n+import re\n+import sys\n+import time\n+from typing import Any\n+from urllib.error import HTTPError, URLError\n+from urllib.request import Request, \"png\"\n+\n+QUALITIES = {\"low\", \"medium\", \"high\", \"auto\"}\n+FORMATS = {\"png\", \"jpeg\", \"jpg\", \"webp\"}\n+BACKGROUNDS = {\"opaque\", \"auto\", \"transparent\"}\n+MODERATION = {\"auto\", print(f\"Error: {message}\", file=sys.stderr)\n+    raise SystemExit(1)\n+\n+\n+def warn(message: str) -> None:\n+    print(f\"Warning: {message}\", file=sys.stderr)\n+\n+\n+def parse_env_file(path: Path) -> dict[str, str]:\n+    values: dict[str, str] = {}\n+    if not path.exists() or not path.is_file():\n+        return values\n+    try:\n+        lines = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\").splitlines()\n+    except UnicodeDecodeError:\n+        return values\n+    for raw in lines:\n+        line = raw.strip()\n+        if not line or line.startswith(\"#\") or \"=\" not in line:\n+            continue\n+        if line.startswith(\"export \"):\n+            line = line[len(\"export \") :].strip()\n+        key, value = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n+        key = key.strip()\n+        value = value.strip().strip('\"').strip(\"'\")\n+        if key:\n+            values[key] = value\n+    return values\n+\n+\n+def candidate_env_files(explicit: list[str]) -> list[Path]:\n+    files: list[Path] = [Path(p).expanduser() for p in explicit]\n+    cwd = Path.cwd()\n+    files.extend(parent / \".env\" for parent in unique: list[Path] = []\n+    for path in files:\n+        resolved = path.expanduser()\n+        if resolved not in seen:\n+            seen.add(resolved)\n+            unique.append(resolved)\n+    return unique\n+\n+\n+def resolve_api_key(explicit_env_files: list[str]) -> str:\n+    existing = os.getenv(\"OPENAI_API_KEY\")\n+    if existing:\n+        return existing\n+    for path in size)\n+    if not match:\n+        return None\n+    return int(match.group(1)), int(match.group(2))\n+\n+\n+def validate_size(size: str, model: str) -> None:\n+    if size == \"auto\":\n+        return\n+    if model != \"gpt-image-2\":\n+        if size not in {\"1024x1024\", \"1536x1024\", \"1024x1536\"}:\n+            die(\"For models before gpt-image-2, size must be 1024x1024, 1536x1024, 1024x1536, or auto.\")\n+        return\n+    parsed = parse_size(size)\n+    if parsed is None:\n+        die(\"size must be auto or WIDTHxHEIGHT, for example 1024x1024\")\n+    width, height = parsed\n+    if width % 16 or height % 16:\n+        die(\"gpt-image-2 size width and height must be multiples of 16px\")\n+    if max(width, height) > GPT_IMAGE_2_MAX_EDGE:\n+ Use when the user explicitly asks for the OpenAI API/CLI path, prompt lists, repeat counts per prompt, dimensions, quality, format, compression, moderation, or saved image files from GPT Image.\"\n+---\n+\n+# GPT Image API Skill\n+\n+Use this skill when the task should call OpenAI's Image API directly instead of the built-in image tool.\n+\n+The canonical Codex copy is:\n+\n+```bash\n+${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills/.system/gpt-image\n+```\n+\n+This Claude copy mirrors the same workflow and script:\n+\n+```bash\n+python \"$HOME/.claude/skills/gpt-image/scripts/gpt_image.py\" generate \\\n+  --prompt \"A clean editorial product photo of a ceramic mug\" \\\n+  --n 2 \\\n+  --size 1024x1024 \\\n+  --quality medium \\\n+  --output-format png \\\n+  --out-dir output/gpt-image\n+```\n+\n+## Current API Notes\n+\n+Official docs checked on 2026-04-30:\n+\n+- Image generation endpoint: `POST /v1/images/generations`.\n+- Latest GPT Image model in the guide: `gpt-image-2`.\n+- Direct generation supports `prompt`, `model`, `n`, `size`, `quality`, `output_format`, `output_compression`, `background`, and `moderation`.\n+- `gpt-image-2` accepts custom `WIDTHxHEIGHT` sizes when both edges are multiples of 16, max edge is <= 3840px, aspect ratio is <= 3:1, and total pixels are 655,360 to 8,294,400.\n+- Popular sizes include `1024x1024`, `1536x1024`, `1024x1536`, `2048x2048`, `2048x1152`, `3840x2160`, `2160x3840`, and `auto`.\n+- `quality`: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `auto`.\n+- `output_format`: `png`, `jpeg`, or `webp`; `output_compression` applies to JPEG/WebP.\n+- `moderation`: `auto` or `low`.\n+- `gpt-image-2` does not support transparent backgrounds.\n+\n+Sources:\n+\n+- https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/image-generation\n+- https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/images\n+\n+## Prompt Lists\n+\n+Use `--prompt` repeatedly or pass `--prompts-file`.\n+\n+Supported prompt files:\n+\n+- Plain text: one prompt per non-empty line.\n+- JSON array: `[\"prompt one\", {\"prompt\": \"prompt two\", \"n\": 3, \"size\": \"1536x1024\"}]`\n+- JSONL: one string or object per line.\n+\n+## API Key Handling\n+\n+Never hardcode or print the API key. The script resolves `OPENAI_API_KEY` from the environment, any explicit `--env-file`, then common local env files including `.env` in the current directory or parents, `$HOME/.codex/.env`, `$HOME/.claude/.env`, and `$HOME/.hermes/.env`.\n+\n+Use `--dry-run` before expensive batches unless the user explicitly asks to execute.\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/.claude/skills/gpt-image/scripts/gpt_image.py\n+#!/usr/bin/env python3\n+\"\"\"Small GPT Image API helper for prompt lists and variants.\n+\n+This file intentionally mirrors:\n+~/.codex/skills/.system/gpt-image/scripts/gpt_image.py\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+from pathlib import Path\n+import runpy\n+\n+CANONICAL = Path.home() / \".codex\" / \"skills\" / \".system\" / \"gpt-image\" / \"scripts\" / \"gpt_image.py\"\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    runpy.run_path(str(CANONICAL), run_name=\"__main__\")\n*** End Patch\n"

  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/SKILL.md\n+---\n+name: \"artagents\"\n+description: \"Use for the ArtAgents repo: file-based hype-cut/video pipeline work, source analysis, transcript/event-talk splitting, pure-generative Remotion timelines, Reigh-compatible timeline/assets JSON, validation/review loops, and GPT Image asset generation via generate_image.py.\"\n+---\n+\n+# ArtAgents\n+\n+ArtAgents is a file-based toolkit for producing Reigh-compatible video edits and generative timelines. Use repo-local CLIs from the repository root; root-level scripts are compatibility launchers for `artagents/*` modules.\n+\n+## First Checks\n+\n+Run these before editing:\n+\n+```bash\n+git status --short\n+rg --files\n+```\n+\n+Do not overwrite unrelated local changes. Large source media and generated artifacts should stay out of git under `runs/` or another ignored output directory.\n+\n+## Core Workflows\n+\n+Source video hype cut:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 pipeline.py --video SRC.mp4 --brief brief.txt --out runs/<name> --render\n+```\n+\n+Audio-only or pure-generative timeline:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 pipeline.py --audio rant.wav --brief brief.txt --out runs/<name> --render\n+python3 pipeline.py --brief brief.txt --theme <theme-id> --out runs/<name> --render --target-duration 28\n+```\n+\n+Event talk splitting:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 transcribe.py --audio talk.wav --out runs/event/transcript --env-file /path/to/.env\n+python3 event_talks.py ados-sunday-template --out runs/event/talks.json\n+python3 event_talks.py search-transcript --transcript runs/event/transcript/transcript.json\n+python3 event_talks.py render --manifest runs/event/talks.json --out-dir runs/event/rendered\n+```\n+\n+## Generate Image Tool\n+\n+Use `generate_image.py` for GPT Image API asset generation inside ArtAgents:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 generate_image.py \\\n+  --prompt \"A minimal editorial still of a red triangle on white\" \\\n+  --n 2 \\\n+  --size 1024x1024 \\\n+  --quality low \\\n+  --output-format png \\\n+  --out-dir runs/images \\\n+  --manifest runs/images/manifest.json\n+```\n+\n+Prompt-list files are supported:\n+\n+- Plain text: one prompt per non-empty line.\n+- JSON array: `[\"prompt one\", {\"prompt\": \"prompt two\", \"n\": 3, \"size\": \"1536x1024\"}]`\n+- JSONL: one string or object per line.\n+\n+Use `--dry-run` before expensive batches. Use `--env-file /path/to/.env` when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is not already available. Do not print or hardcode API keys.\n+\n+Current GPT Image defaults:\n+\n+- model: `gpt-image-2`\n+- size: `1024x1024`\n+- quality: `medium`\n+- output format: `png`\n+\n+`gpt-image-2` size rules: `WIDTHxHEIGHT` must use multiples of 16, max edge <= 3840, aspect ratio <= 3:1, and total pixels between 655,360 and 8,294,400.\n+\n+## Important Contracts\n+\n+- `pipeline.py --brief ...` is the canonical top-level flow.\n+- `cut.py` emits `hype.timeline.json`, `hype.assets.json`, `hype.metadata.json`, and `hype.edl.csv`.\n+- Reigh-facing JSON should round-trip through existing helpers and tests.\n+- Source-cut timelines preserve legacy `clipType=\"text\"` overlays.\n+- Pure-generative timelines may use extended `clipType` values and `params`.\n+- Effects live under workspace-level `effects/<id>/` or theme-level `themes/<id>/effects/<id>/`.\n+- After adding or renaming effects, run:\n+\n+```bash\n+cd remotion\n+npm run gen-types\n+```\n+\n+## Validation\n+\n+Use focused tests for changed behavior:\n+\n+```bash\n+pytest tests/test_generate_image.py\n+pytest tests/test_schema_contract.py tests/test_pure_generative_pipeline.py\n+```\n+\n+For render-related changes, also run the relevant Remotion or render smoke tests already in `tests/`.\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/agents/openai.yaml\n+interface:\n+  display_name: \"ArtAgents\"\n+  short_description: \"Video pipeline and GPT Image asset tooling\"\n+  default_prompt: \"Use $artagents to generate a Reigh-compatible edit or create GPT Image assets for an ArtAgents run.\"\n+policy:\n+  allow_implicit_invocation: true\n*** End Patch\n"
}

Slice cells into `frames/frame_001.png`, `frames/frame_002.png`, etc.\n+7. Assemble `sprite_preview.mp4` for quick review and `sprite_preview_prores.mov` as a higher-quality animation master.\n+8. > AGENT

Built and tested the ArtAgents `Sprite Sheet` setting end to end.

Added:

- [sprite_sheet.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/sprite_sheet.py) root launcher
- [artagents/sprite_sheet.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/sprite_sheet.py)
- [tests/test_sprite_sheet.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/tests/test_sprite_sheet.py)
- Updated [SKILL.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/SKILL.md) with a first-class `Sprite Sheet Setting`
- Updated [README.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/README.md)

What it does:

1. Computes exact sheet dimensions from `cols * frame_width` and `rows * frame_height`.
2. Writes `layout_guide.png` with grid/safe-area outlines.
3. Calls GPT Image using that layout guide through `/v1/images/edits`.
4. Saves `sprite_sheet.png`.
5. Validates the returned PNG dimensions exactly.
6. Slices frames into `frames/frame_001.png`, etc.
7. Builds `sprite_preview.mp4` for review.
8. Builds `sprite_preview_prores.mov` as the higher-quality master.
9. Writes `sprite_manifest.json` with prompt, layout, frame positions, outputs, usage.

Live test output:

- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-live/layout_guide.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-live/sprite_sheet.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-live/frames/frame_001.png` through `frame_004.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-live/sprite_preview.mp4`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-live/sprite_preview_prores.mov`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-live/sprite_manifest.json`

I opened the generated sprite sheet, MP4, and ProRes MOV for you. The sheet came back cleanly as a 2x2 `1024x1024` PNG with no visible guide lines, and the frames were sliced/assembled successfully.

Validation run:

> AGENT

Yes, that is possible, and for sprite sheets it is usually the most deterministic route. I’m going to wire it as: generate on a flat chroma color by default, remove that color with ffmpeg into an alpha PNG, slice the transparent sheet, and make the review video over a checkerboard/white background while keeping a ProRes alpha master.

> AGENT

The docs and quick tests suggest three viable modes: native transparent on `gpt-image-1.5`, chroma-key removal for `gpt-image-2`, and a third-party RMBG model for difficult real-world edges. I’m making chroma-key the sprite-sheet default because it preserves the exact grid and is easiest to validate frame by frame.

The alpha removal actually worked: about 850k pixels are fully transparent. The viewer here is showing the hidden RGB values under transparent pixels, so I’m adding a cleanup pass that zeroes RGB where alpha is zero. That makes the PNG behave better in tools that ignore alpha.

> TOOL

tool_use exec_command
```json
{
  "cmd": "python3 - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport struct,zlib\np=Path('/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/sprite_sheet_alpha.png')\ndata=p.read_bytes(); pos=8; idat=[]; w=h=bd=ct=None\nwhile pos < len(data):\n l=struct.unpack('>I',data[pos:pos+4])[0]; k=data[pos+4:pos+8]; c=data[pos+8:pos+8+l]; pos += 12+l\n if k==b'IHDR': w,h,bd,ct,*_=struct.unpack('>IIBBBBB',c)\n elif k==b'IDAT': idat.append(c)\n elif k==b'IEND': break\nraw=zlib.decompress(b''.join(idat))\n# implement PNG unfilter for RGBA8\nbpp=4; stride=w*bpp; prev=bytearray(stride); alphas=[]; rgbs=[]; i=0\nfor y in range(h):\n f=raw[i]; i+=1; scan=bytearray(raw[i:i+stride]); i+=stride; out=bytearray(stride)\n for x in range(stride):\n  left=out[x-bpp] if x>=bpp else 0\n  up=prev[x]\n  ul=prev[x-bpp] if x>=bpp else 0\n  if f==0: val=scan[x]\n  elif f==1: val=(scan[x]+left)&255\n  elif f==2: val=(scan[x]+up)&255\n  elif f==3: val=(scan[x]+((left+up)//2))&255\n  elif f==4:\n   p=left+up-ul; pa=abs(p-left); pb=abs(p-up); pc=abs(p-ul); pr=left if pa<=pb and pa<=pc else (up if pb<=pc else ul)\n   val=(scan[x]+pr)&255\n  else: raise ValueError(f)\n  out[x]=val\n prev=out\n alphas.extend(out[3::4])\n rgbs.extend(tuple(out[j:j+3]) for j in range(0,stride,4))\nprint('alpha_min', min(alphas), 'alpha_max', max(alphas), 'transparent', sum(a==0 for a in alphas), 'partial', sum(0<a<255 for a in alphas), 'opaque', sum(a==255 for a in alphas))\n# count exact/near magenta opaque\nnear=0; near_trans=0\nfor (r,g,b),a in zip(rgbs, alphas):\n if abs(r-255)<=10 and abs(g-0)<=10 and abs(b-255)<=10:\n  near += 1\n  if a==0: near_trans += 1\nprint('near_magenta', near, 'near_magenta_transparent', near_trans)\nPY",

> AGENT

Yes. I tested the options:

Prompt-only transparency with `gpt-image-2` did **not** create real alpha. It returned RGB PNG, no alpha channel.

Native transparency with `gpt-image-1.5` **did** work using `--background transparent`: RGBA PNG with alpha.

For `gpt-image-2` sprite sheets, I implemented the more reliable fallback: chroma-key generation and removal.

What changed:

- `sprite_sheet.py` now defaults to transparent output.
- It prompts GPT Image to use a flat magenta `#ff00ff` chroma background.
- It removes that with ffmpeg into `sprite_sheet_alpha.png`.
- It slices transparent RGBA frames.
- It creates review MP4 over a background plus ProRes MOV with alpha.
- It also scrubs hidden RGB values where alpha is zero so transparent PNGs behave better in tools.

Tested output:

- `/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/sprite_sheet.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/sprite_sheet_alpha.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/sprite_sheet_alpha_stronger.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/frames/frame_001.png` etc.
- `/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/sprite_preview_stronger.mp4`
- `/tmp/artagents-transparent-sprite-live/sprite_preview_prores_stronger.mov`

The alpha check passed: `sprite_sheet_alpha_stronger.png` is RGBA with ~852k fully transparent pixels. I opened the original sheet, white-preview image, MP4, and ProRes MOV for you.

For external removal: fal has Bria RMBG 2.0 and rembg endpoints that return PNGs with alpha. That’s probably best for photos, hair, smoke, translucent edges, or non-controlled backgrounds. For generated sprite sheets, chroma key is usually better because it preserves the exact grid and is deterministic.

Sources checked:

- OpenAI image docs: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/image-generation
- fal Bria background removal: https://fal.ai/docs/model-api-reference/image-generation-api/bria-background
- fal rembg: https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/imageutils/rembg/api

> AGENT

You were right: the cut looked wrong because the generated sheet itself violated the cell boundaries. The slicing grid was mathematically correct, but GPT placed the character too close to, or across, the top/bottom edges in many cells.

I measured alpha bounds for all 30 frames:

- Raw sliced frames: `29 / 30` touched the safety edge.
- Some frames had alpha content spanning `y=0` to `y=255`, which means the character was effectively crossing the crop boundary.
- After normalization: `0 / 30` frames touch the safety edge.

I added a fix:

- The tool now analyzes sliced frame alpha bounds.
- It writes `edge_warning_count` into `sprite_manifest.json`.
- It supports `--normalize-frames --normalize-margin 18`.
- Normalization crops to alpha content, scales down only if needed, and recenters into the original frame size.

Tested on the 30-frame sheet:

```bash
python3 sprite_sheet.py \
  --input-sheet /tmp/artagents-sprite-30/sprite_sheet.png \
  --animation "post-process and normalize existing 30-frame run-jump-loop" \
  --subject "adventurous explorer character" \
  --frames 30 \
  --frame-width 256 \
  --frame-height 256 \
  --fps 12 \
  --key-similarity 0.18 \
  --key-blend 0.08 \
  --normalize-frames \
  --normalize-margin 18 \
  --out-dir /tmp/artagents-sprite-30-normalized \
  --force
```

Opened for you:

- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-normalized/sprite_preview.mp4`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-normalized/sprite_preview_prores.mov`
- representative normalized frames 1 and 30

This is probably the right default for imperfect GPT sprite sheets: generate with strict cell instructions, then validate and normalize frames when the sheet drifts.

> DEVELOPER

And do we dynamically generate a layout guide for each image? How does that work?

> AGENT

Yes. For each sprite-sheet request, the tool computes the grid first, then writes a matching `layout_guide.png` into that run’s output directory before calling GPT Image. The guide dimensions exactly match the requested sheet dimensions, and the cell count/safe margin are derived from the requested frame count and frame size.

> AGENT

Yes, we dynamically generate a layout guide for every sprite-sheet request.

How it works now:

- You specify `--frames`, `--frame-width`, `--frame-height`, optionally `--cols` / `--rows`.
- The tool chooses a valid grid under `gpt-image-2` limits if rows/cols are omitted.
- It writes a matching `layout_guide.png` before calling GPT Image.
- That guide is exactly the final sheet size.
- It includes the exact grid, safe boxes, center marks, chroma background, and cell boundaries.
- The prompt then references the computed canvas, grid, frame count, cell size, and safe margin.

I also used the ArtAgents vision tool as requested. It confirmed the issue on the old 30-frame run: frames around the flip/landing had disconnected or clipped body parts. That was not the slicer using wrong coordinates; GPT generated content across/near cell boundaries. The old guide existed, but it was too weak and the `256x256` cells were too tight for this complex animation.

I improved the guide and reran a stronger 30-frame test:

- New output: `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-v2`
- Layout: `5 x 6`
- Frame size: `512 x 512`
- Sheet size: `2560 x 3072`
- Safe margin: `64px`
- Raw edge warnings: `0`
- Normalized edge touches: `0`

Opened for you:

- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-v2/layout_guide.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-v2/sprite_sheet.png`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-v2/sprite_preview.mp4`
- `/tmp/artagents-sprite-30-v2/sprite_preview_prores.mov`

This is the better default for complex characters: larger cells, stronger guide, explicit safe area, and validation after slicing.

> DEVELOPER

And if we want to upscale this animation, what would be the best stage at which to do that? While maintaining the background and working with the various things. Say if we wanted to make it really big, we could use an upscaler on file, either a video or image depending on what makes sense. We can upscale the individual images. The key thing is that we're still able to remove the background properly and it still works with all those various settings throughout it. Can you think about that please?

> AGENT

For upscaling, the best stage is after background removal and slicing, on the normalized transparent frame PNGs. Upscaling the whole keyed sheet before background removal risks making the chroma edge harder to remove; upscaling the final MP4 bakes in a background and loses the alpha workflow.

> AGENT

The current web export is taking longer than expected because animated WebP encoding from 30 large RGBA frames is expensive. I’m going to let it finish, but for upscaling I’d add a separate optional `--upscale-frames` stage that runs before web/export formats.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: artagents/sprite_sheet.py\n@@\n def upscale_frames(frames: list[str], out_dir: Path, *, factor: float, filter_name: str, force: bool) -> list[str]:\n@@\n     return outputs\n \n \n+def _workspace_env_files(env_file: Path | None) -> list[Path]:\n+    candidates = _candidate_env_files(env_file)\n+    repo_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\n+    workspace = repo_root.parent\n+    candidates.extend(\n+        [\n+            Path.cwd() / \".env\",\n+            workspace / \".env\",\n+            workspace / \"reigh-app\" / \".env\",\n+            workspace / \"reigh-worker\" / \".env\",\n+            workspace / \"reigh-worker-orchestrator\" / \".env\",\n+        ]\n+    )\n+    seen: set[Path] = set()\n+    unique: list[Path] = []\n+    for candidate in candidates:\n+        resolved = candidate.expanduser().resolve()\n+        if resolved not in seen:\n+            seen.add(resolved)\n+            unique.append(resolved)\n+    return unique\n+\n+\n+def load_fal_key(env_file: Path | None = None) -> str:\n+    for key_name in FAL_KEY_NAMES:\n+        if key := os.environ.get(key_name, \"\").strip():\n+            return key\n+    tried: list[str] = [\", \".join(FAL_KEY_NAMES) + \" environment variables\"]\n+    for candidate in _workspace_env_files(env_file):\n+        tried.append(str(candidate))\n+        for key_name in FAL_KEY_NAMES:\n+            if key := _read_env_value(candidate, key_name):\n+                return key\n+    raise SystemExit(f\"FAL_KEY or FAL_API_KEY not found. Tried: {', '.join(tried)}\")\n+\n+\n+def _download_url(url: str, output_path: Path, *, force: bool, timeout: int) -> None:\n+    if output_path.exists() and not force:\n+        _die(f\"Output exists: {output_path} (use --force to overwrite)\")\n+    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+    request = Request(url, headers={\"User-Agent\": \"ArtAgents/1.0\"})\n+    try:\n+        with urlopen(request, timeout=timeout) as response:\n+            output_path.write_bytes(response.read())\n+    except (HTTPError, URLError, TimeoutError) as exc:\n+        _die(f\"Failed to download FAL output: {exc}\")\n+\n+\n+def _fal_image_url(result: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n+    image = result.get(\"image\")\n+    if isinstance(image, dict) and isinstance(image.get(\"url\"), str):\n+        return image[\"url\"]\n+    images = result.get(\"images\")\n+    if isinstance(images, list) and images:\n+        first = images[0]\n+        if isinstance(first, dict) and isinstance(first.get(\"url\"), str):\n+            return first[\"url\"]\n+        if isinstance(first, str):\n+            return first\n+    _die(\"FAL upscaler result did not include an image URL\")\n+    return \"\"\n+\n+\n+def _ai_upscale_prompt(args: argparse.Namespace) -> str:\n+    if args.ai_upscale_prompt:\n+        return args.ai_upscale_prompt\n+    return (\n+        f\"High-quality AI upscaling for a transparent game sprite frame. Preserve the exact pose, silhouette, animation timing, \"\n+        f\"character identity, and clean 2D sprite style. Subject: {args.subject}. Animation: {args.animation}.\"\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def _merge_upscaled_rgb_with_source_alpha(source_frame: Path, upscaled_rgb: Path, output_path: Path, *, factor: float, force: bool) -> None:\n+    width, height, _ = _read_rgba_png(source_frame)\n+    target_width = max(1, int(round(width * factor)))\n+    target_height = max(1, int(round(height * factor)))\n+    if output_path.exists() and not force:\n+        _die(f\"Output exists: {output_path} (use --force to overwrite)\")\n+    output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+    _run(\n+        [\n+            \"ffmpeg\",\n+            \"-hide_banner\",\n+            \"-loglevel\",\n+            \"error\",\n+            \"-y\" if force else \"-n\",\n+            \"-i\",\n+            str(upscaled_rgb),\n+            \"-i\",\n+            str(source_frame),\n+            \"-filter_complex\",\n+            (\n+                f\"[0:v]scale={target_width}:{target_height}:flags=lanczos,format=rgb24[rgb];\"\n+                f\"[1:v]alphaextract,scale={target_width}:{target_height}:flags=lanczos[alpha];\"\n+                \"[rgb][alpha]alphamerge,format=rgba[out]\"\n+            ),\n+            \"-map\",\n+            \"[out]\",\n+            \"-frames:v\",\n+            \"1\",\n+            str(output_path),\n+        ]\n+    )\n+    scrub_fully_transparent_rgb(output_path)\n+\n+\n+def ai_upscale_frames_with_fal(\n+    frames: list[str],\n+    out_dir: Path,\n+    *,\n+    args: argparse.Namespace,\n+    force: bool,\n+) -> tuple[list[str], list[dict[str, Any]]]:\n+    try:\n+        import fal_client\n+    except ImportError:\n+        _die(\"fal-client is required for --ai-upscale-provider fal. Install requirements or run: pip install fal-client\")\n+\n+    fal_key = load_fal_key(args.fal_env_file or args.env_file)\n+    client = fal_client.SyncClient(key=fal_key, default_timeout=float(args.fal_timeout))\n+    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+    raw_dir = out_dir / \"fal_raw\"\n+    raw_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+    prompt = _ai_upscale_prompt(args)\n+    outputs: list[str] = []\n+    reports: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n+    for index, frame in enumerate(frames, start=1):\n+        source_frame = Path(frame)\n+        raw_output = raw_dir / f\"frame_{index:03d}.png\"\n+        final_output = out_dir / f\"frame_{index:03d}.png\"\n+        print(f\"FAL upscaling frame {index}/{len(frames)} with {args.ai_upscale_model}\", file=sys.stderr)\n+        image_url = client.upload_file(source_frame)\n+        payload = {\n+            \"image_url\": image_url,\n+            \"prompt\": prompt,\n+            \"upscale_factor\": args.ai_upscale_factor,\n+            \"negative_prompt\": args.ai_upscale_negative_prompt,\n+            \"creativity\": args.ai_upscale_creativity,\n+            \"resemblance\": args.ai_upscale_resemblance,\n+            \"guidance_scale\": args.ai_upscale_guidance_scale,\n+            \"num_inference_steps\": args.ai_upscale_steps,\n+            \"enable_safety_checker\": True,\n+        }\n+        result = client.subscribe(\n+            args.ai_upscale_model,\n+            arguments=payload,\n+            with_logs=args.fal_logs,\n+            client_timeout=float(args.fal_timeout),\n+        )\n+        output_url = _fal_image_url(result)\n+        _download_url(output_url, raw_output, force=force, timeout=args.fal_timeout)\n+        _merge_upscaled_rgb_with_source_alpha(\n+            source_frame,\n+            raw_output,\n+            final_output,\n+            factor=args.ai_upscale_factor,\n+            force=force,\n+        )\n+        outputs.append(str(final_output))\n+        reports.append(\n+            {\n+                \"frame\": index,\n+                \"source\": str(source_frame),\n+                \"raw_output\": str(raw_output),\n+                \"output\": str(final_output),\n+                \"model\": args.ai_upscale_model,\n+                \"upscale_factor\": args.ai_upscale_factor,\n+                \"seed\": result.get(\"seed\") if isinstance(result, dict) else None,\n+            }\n+        )\n+    return outputs, reports\n+\n+\n def _parse_hex_color(raw: str) -> tuple[int, int, int]:\n*** End Patch\n"
}

Use `--normalize-frames --normalize-margin 18` to crop each frame to its alpha bounds, scale down only when necessary, and recenter it in the same frame size before building previews.\n+\n+For proper high-quality upscaling, upscale after transparency extraction and normalization, not before slicing and not from the final video. Use FAL Clarity Upscaler:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 sprite_sheet.py \\\n+  --input-sheet runs/sprites/star-idle/sprite_sheet.png \\\n+  --animation \"existing 30-frame run cycle\" \\\n+  --subject \"same character\" \\\n+  --frames 30 \\\n+  --frame-width 512 \\\n+  --frame-height 512 \\\n+  --fps 12 \\\n+  --normalize-frames \\\n+  --ai-upscale-provider fal \\\n+  --ai-upscale-factor 2 \\\n+  --out-dir runs/sprites/star-idle-ai-upscaled \\\n+  --force\n+```\n+\n+The FAL path uploads each transparent PNG frame to `fal-ai/clarity-upscaler`, downloads the enhanced result, then re-applies an upscaled copy of the original alpha mask so the background remains clean. Defaults are tuned for sprite preservation: low creativity and high resemblance. It reads `FAL_KEY` or `FAL_API_KEY` from the process, `--fal-env-file`, or nearby workspace `.env` files. Do not print or hardcode FAL keys.\n+\n+The local `--upscale-factor` path is only a deterministic ffmpeg fallback. Use it for fast previews, not final high-quality enlargement.\n+\n+For web delivery, prefer the default WebP atlas plus `sprite_web_manifest.json` for CSS/canvas playback, or individual WebP frames for lazy-loaded animation systems. Use `--web-max-dim` to cap runtime frame size and `--web-quality` to tune file size. Animated WebP is opt-in with `--web-animated`; for 20-30 frame sprites it is usually less efficient and less controllable than atlas/canvas playback.\n*** End Patch\n"

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: README.md\n@@\n By default, sprite sheets are generated on a flat chroma-key background and converted to `sprite_sheet_alpha.png` plus alpha PNG frames. Use `--no-transparent --background \"...\"` to keep a visible background.\n@@\n If the generated art drifts across cell boundaries, add `--normalize-frames --normalize-margin 18`. The tool will warn via `edge_warning_count` in `sprite_manifest.json` when sliced content touches frame edges.\n+\n+For final-size assets, upscale the transparent frame sequence after slicing and normalization:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 sprite_sheet.py \\\n+  --input-sheet runs/sprites/star-idle/sprite_sheet.png \\\n+  --animation \"existing 30-frame run cycle\" \\\n+  --subject \"same character\" \\\n+  --frames 30 \\\n+  --frame-width 512 \\\n+  --frame-height 512 \\\n+  --fps 12 \\\n+  --normalize-frames \\\n+  --ai-upscale-provider fal \\\n+  --ai-upscale-factor 2 \\\n+  --out-dir runs/sprites/star-idle-ai-upscaled \\\n+  --force\n+```\n+\n+`--ai-upscale-provider fal` uses FAL's Clarity Upscaler (`fal-ai/clarity-upscaler`) with low creativity and high resemblance defaults so the frame pose and silhouette are preserved. The tool re-applies the original upscaled alpha mask to the AI result, which keeps transparent-background sprites clean. `--upscale-factor` without `--ai-upscale-provider` is only a fast local ffmpeg fallback.\n+\n+Web exports are on by default and write `web/sprite_sheet.webp`, `web/frames/*.webp`, `web/sprite_preview_web.mp4`, and `web/sprite_web_manifest.json`. Use the atlas or frame sequence for efficient CSS/canvas playback; animated WebP is opt-in with `--web-animated` because large animated sprites are usually heavier and harder to control at runtime.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> AGENT

Yes. I wired it so the high-quality path and web path are separate:

- Proper AI upscale: `--ai-upscale-provider fal` uses FAL `fal-ai/clarity-upscaler`, which FAL describes as a high-fidelity image upscaler and exposes params like `upscale_factor`, `creativity`, `resemblance`, `guidance_scale`, and steps. Source: https://fal.ai/models/fal-ai/clarity-upscaler/api
- Transparency stays clean: the tool upscales each transparent PNG frame, then reapplies an upscaled copy of the original alpha mask.
- Web optimized output: it now rebuilds a processed atlas from the final frames, then exports:
  - `web/sprite_sheet.webp`
  - `web/frames/*.webp`
  - `web/sprite_preview_web.mp4`
  - `web/sprite_web_manifest.json`
- Animated WebP is now opt-in via `--web-animated`, because for 20-30 frames the atlas or individual WebP frames are usually faster and more controllable on the web.

I tested a live FAL smoke run on 2 frames. Output frames became `1024x1024`, alpha was preserved, and edge analysis showed no boundary touches. The web smoke export produced a `2048x1024` processed atlas and compact WebP/MP4 outputs. I also opened the upscaled frame, preview video, web atlas, and web preview for you.

Changed docs/code:
- [artagents/sprite_sheet.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/sprite_sheet.py)
- [SKILL.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/SKILL.md)
- [README.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/README.md)
- [requirements.txt](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/requirements.txt)

Validation: `pytest tests/test_sprite_sheet.py tests/test_generate_image.py` passed, 8 tests.

> DEVELOPER

Nice, is there an audio understanding model we could use - not just words but depeer understanding - do do something similar with audio?

> AGENT

I’ll check current official model docs before answering, because audio understanding model support changes quickly and the useful distinction here is transcription versus direct audio reasoning.

> AGENT

Yes. For “deeper than words” audio understanding, the right OpenAI model is `gpt-audio`, not just `gpt-4o-transcribe`.

The distinction:

- `gpt-4o-transcribe` / `gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize`: best for transcripts, speaker labels, timestamps.
- `gpt-audio`: accepts audio directly and can reason over what it hears, so it is better for tone, emotion, pacing, music/sound cues, speaker energy, awkward pauses, emphasis, audience reaction, audio quality, etc.
- `gpt-realtime`: similar audio-native direction, but for live/streaming interaction.

For ArtAgents, I’d build an `audio_understand.py` tool similar to `visual_understand.py`:

1. Slice audio/video into sampled windows, e.g. 10-30 sec chunks.
2. Send chunks directly to `gpt-audio` with a structured query.
3. Ask for JSON like:
   - `energy`
   - `emotion`
   - `speaker_confidence`
   - `audience_reaction`
   - `music_or_sfx`
   - `silence_or_dead_air`
   - `highlight_score`
   - `clip_boundary_suggestions`
   - `editor_notes`
4. Optionally combine that with transcript text from `gpt-4o-transcribe-diarize`.

Best practical setup: transcript + direct audio reasoning. Transcript gives accurate words and speaker turns; `gpt-audio` gives the feel of the moment. For hype cuts, talk splitting, trailer pacing, and “find the good bits,” that combination is much stronger than transcription alone.

Sources: OpenAI lists `gpt-audio` as the generally available audio input/output model, and transcription models separately as speech-to-text models.  
https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-audio  
https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/audio  
https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/speech-to-text/prompting

> DEVELOPER

can you do the exact same skill/capability as we just did but applied to audio? Think through it philosophically

> AGENT

I’ll add this as an ArtAgents capability rather than just describe it: an `audio_understand.py` tool, skill/docs updates, and focused tests. The design goal is the same as the visual tool: make audio queryable as evidence, but preserve the distinction between transcript facts and direct listening judgments.

> AGENT

I’ve got the API shape now. OpenAI’s docs explicitly say audio is not supported in Responses API yet, so this tool should use Chat Completions with `gpt-audio` and base64 `input_audio`, while transcription stays on the transcription endpoint.

> DEVELOPER

For example, for comparing them it should say number one, you know, then play the audio. Number two, play the audio. Number three and it could be like, you know, which quote sounds the most emotional or which one sounds the most exciting or are they speaking fast enough here and stuff like that.

> AGENT

That changes the shape in a useful way: the audio equivalent of a visual contact sheet should be an audition reel, not separate unrelated API calls. I’m adapting the tool so comparisons can be presented as one labeled sequence: “Number one”, clip, “Number two”, clip, and then the model judges across them.

> AGENT

The implementation now follows your example directly: repeated `--audio` clips become a numbered reel, so the model hears “Number 1” then the clip, “Number 2” then the clip, etc. That gives it the right comparative context for questions like emotionality, excitement, speaking pace, and whether the delivery works.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: SKILL.md\n@@\n ## Visual Understanding Tool\n@@\n Store source-derived analysis at asset level. `hype.metadata.json` already uses `sources[asset_key].transcript_ref`, `scenes_ref`, `shots_ref`, and `quality_zones_ref`; boundary candidate packages should mirror that with `asset_key`, `asset_analysis`, and `metadata_source_refs`. Do not embed this analysis into `hype.timeline.json` or `hype.assets.json`; those stay Reigh-compatible and timelines only reference assets and edits.\n \n+## Audio Understanding Tool\n+\n+Use `audio_understand.py` when the edit question depends on how something sounds, not just what was said: emotional force, excitement, speed, pauses, laughter, applause, music/SFX, clipping, echo, room tone, or whether a quote has a clean in/out.\n+\n+Single source:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 audio_understand.py \\\n+  --audio quote.wav \\\n+  --query \"Is this quote emotionally strong enough for an opener? Is the speaker too slow?\" \\\n+  --out runs/audio-understanding/quote-review.json \\\n+  --env-file /path/to/.env\n+```\n+\n+Comparison sources:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 audio_understand.py \\\n+  --audio quote-a.wav \\\n+  --audio quote-b.wav \\\n+  --audio quote-c.wav \\\n+  --query \"Which numbered quote sounds the most emotional and exciting? Rank them and flag any pacing problems.\" \\\n+  --out runs/audio-understanding/quote-comparison.json\n+```\n+\n+For comparison, the tool builds a numbered audition reel: it says \"Number 1\", plays the first clip, says \"Number 2\", plays the second clip, and so on. This is the audio equivalent of a visual contact sheet: the model hears the candidates in one context and can compare delivery, pace, intensity, and production quality directly.\n+\n+For video, pass `--video source.mp4 --at 01:20,03:45 --window-sec 20` to extract windows around candidate moments. Multiple windows also default to a numbered audition reel. Use `--audition-reel never` when you want per-window independent analysis.\n+\n+Philosophical rule: transcript is factual text evidence; direct audio understanding is listening evidence. Use `transcribe.py` for exact words and speaker timing, then use `audio_understand.py` for embodied editorial judgment: urgency, hesitation, charm, tension, crowd response, music shape, and whether the cut feels alive.\n+\n ## Sprite Sheet Setting\n*** End Patch\n"
}

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: README.md\n@@\n ### Visual understanding\n \n `visual_understand.py` asks an OpenAI vision model about a single image or a numbered contact sheet built from multiple images/video frames. It defaults to `--mode fast` using `gpt-4o-mini`, `detail=low`, four columns, and a hard cap of 20 images so review queries stay cheap and bounded.\n@@\n `boundary_candidates.py` writes standardized candidate frame sets with `asset_key`, `asset_analysis`, and `metadata_source_refs` fields. These refs follow the same asset-level pattern as `hype.metadata.json` `sources[asset_key]`, so timelines can point at the analysis without embedding source analysis into `hype.timeline.json` or `hype.assets.json`.\n \n+### Audio understanding\n+\n+`audio_understand.py` is the audio-native companion to `visual_understand.py`. Use it when the relevant evidence is delivery, emotion, pace, audience reaction, music/SFX, room sound, clipping, or edit feel rather than exact transcript text.\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 audio_understand.py \\\n+  --audio quote.wav \\\n+  --query \"Is this quote emotionally strong enough for an opener? Is the speaker too slow?\" \\\n+  --out runs/audio-understanding/quote-review.json\n+```\n+\n+For comparing candidate quotes, repeat `--audio`. The tool builds a numbered audition reel, literally introducing each candidate before playing it, so the model can compare them in one listening pass:\n+\n+```bash\n+python3 audio_understand.py \\\n+  --audio quote-a.wav \\\n+  --audio quote-b.wav \\\n+  --audio quote-c.wav \\\n+  --query \"Which numbered quote sounds the most emotional and exciting? Rank them and flag pacing problems.\" \\\n+  --out runs/audio-understanding/quote-comparison.json\n+```\n+\n+For video source moments, use `--video source.mp4 --at 01:20,03:45 --window-sec 20`. Multiple windows also become a numbered audition reel by default. Use `--audition-reel never` for independent per-window analysis.\n+\n+The intended division of labor is: `transcribe.py` supplies exact words and timestamps; `audio_understand.py` supplies listening judgment. This keeps \"what was said\" separate from \"how it landed.\"\n+\n ## Event Talk Splitting\n*** End Patch\n"
}

Implemented the audio version as a real ArtAgents capability.

Added [artagents/audio_understand.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/audio_understand.py) plus root launcher [audio_understand.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/audio_understand.py). It supports:

- Single audio/video-window listening analysis.
- Repeated `--audio` clips for comparison.
- Numbered audition reels: “Number 1”, clip, “Number 2”, clip, etc.
- Queries like “which quote sounds most emotional/exciting?” or “is the speaker too slow?”
- `--video source.mp4 --at 01:20,03:45 --window-sec 20` for candidate moments from video.
- `--audition-reel never` for independent per-window analysis.

The philosophical split is now explicit in [SKILL.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/SKILL.md): `transcribe.py` is for exact words/timestamps; `audio_understand.py` is for listening evidence: emotion, pace, confidence, room feel, laughter/applause, music/SFX, clipping, dead air, and edit value.

I also updated [README.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/README.md) and added [tests/test_audio_understand.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/tests/test_audio_understand.py).

Verified:
- Unit/integration group: `14 passed` using the repo venv.
- Live `gpt-audio-mini` smoke test with a numbered audition reel worked. After hardening the prompt to ignore spoken number labels, it correctly identified candidate `2` as the higher-pitched clip.

Docs basis: OpenAI’s audio guide currently points audio input through Chat Completions with audio-capable models, while transcription remains separate.  
https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/audio

> DEVELOPER

And is that definitely how the audio understanding model works?

> AGENT

I’ll verify against current official docs rather than relying on memory, because the audio API surface has moved between Responses, Chat Completions, Realtime, and transcription endpoints.

> AGENT

Yes, with one caveat.

The API shape is correct: OpenAI’s docs say `gpt-audio` and `gpt-audio-mini` are audio-capable models for audio input/output through Chat Completions, and the audio guide says audio is not yet supported in the Responses API. So using `/v1/chat/completions` with `input_audio` is the right path.

What it can do:
- Listen to audio directly, not only read a transcript.
- Judge tone, pace, emotion, emphasis, excitement, pauses, sound quality, music/SFX, etc.
- Compare clips if we package them as a numbered audition reel.

What it is not:
- It is not a deterministic audio-analysis DSP tool.
- It may misjudge subtle acoustic details unless the clips are clean and the prompt is explicit.
- For exact words/timestamps, transcription models are still the right layer.

So the architecture is sound: `transcribe.py` for exact language; `audio_understand.py` for direct listening judgment. The live smoke test also confirmed the endpoint accepts the numbered reel and reasons over the audio.

Sources:
- `gpt-audio`: https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-audio
- `gpt-audio-mini`: https://platform.openai.com/docs/models/gpt-audio-mini
- Audio guide: https://platform.openai.com/docs/guides/audio

> DEVELOPER

Did you actually test this?

> AGENT

Yes. I tested both the local tool behavior and a live API call.

What I ran:
- Unit tests for the new audio tool: `tests/test_audio_understand.py`
- Broader focused test group through the repo venv: `14 passed`
- A live `gpt-audio-mini` call using two generated WAV tones packaged as a numbered audition reel.

The live test initially exposed a real issue: the spoken “Number 1 / Number 2” labels could contaminate audio judgments. I patched the prompt to explicitly ignore spoken number labels and added a short silence after each label. After that, the live test correctly identified candidate `2` as the higher-pitched clip.

Output path from the live test:
`/tmp/artagents-audio-understand-smoke2/result.json`
